# Stdio Oracle Composition Design — 2026-10-01

Read-only design based on existing sources and the supplied completed gate. No production edits, commands that compile, or test execution. Proposed paths below are NEW planned owners, not claims that files already exist.

## Decision and Scope

Move the authored artifact oracle composition root out of plugin-root Stdio into **`✏️s/🧪️tests/🔮️oracles/🧩️composition`**, with native package `📦️packages/🦀️rust` named **`semio-s-test-oracle-composition`** and library `semio_s_test_oracle_composition`. The composition crate is an S integration owner; its composition mechanism is neutral, while its admitted contributions remain actual S artifact owners. It is not a framework module and must not be presented as one. Framework test runtime stays free of artifact names and external dependencies.

Relocate the existing transparent `artifacts::<format>::standards::<version>::subsets::<subset>` authored module tree into that composition source. Mount the same 72 exact leaf-owned oracle sources, with recalculated relative literals. This removes the lower plugin-root → artifact mounts without moving subset oracle implementations out of their real owners. Reexport paths within the new crate can retain the current semantic tree; the **old crate name/path must disappear entirely**, with all consuming authored adapters/descriptors updated together. No legacy alias, facade at the old owner, generated producer rewrite, test exemption, or broad physical rule relaxation.

Shared format-family implementations (document/raster/archive/audio/tabular/mesh/markup) can remain at their present plugin-root oracle owners: they implement shared reference algorithms rather than importing artifact leaves. The higher S crate mounts them alongside actual subset contributions; their existing `crate::document`-style sibling references remain meaningful in the new compilation root. Longer-term independent leaf packages can use an explicit shared owned interface, but 72 new packages are not necessary to correct this source-direction cut.

Extract **only truly domain-neutral law/vector machinery** from Stdio `🔮️oracles/⚖️law` into the existing dependency-free repository test host owner, e.g. `🧰️framework/🛍️products/🦑️repo/🔨️modules/🧪️test/⚖️law/🦀️.rs`, exported by `semio_repo_test_host::law`. Its comparison, inversion, observability, JSON divergence and vector APIs already use the host's owned `Json`/adapter values. Update cross-plugin law consumers and path-mounted law modules to use this lower neutral API, rather than making unrelated plugin tests compile all S artifact oracles. S composition may use this interface, but must not reexport a legacy law alias. Review all child law files before moving; no format-specific implementation may be lowered.

## Actual Current Package and Descriptor Authority

- `✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust/Cargo.toml:1-18` is a standalone `[workspace]` package, library path `../../🦀️.rs`, and `oracles` feature. Keep standalone workspace behavior to avoid shared root manifest contention.
- The `oracles` feature enables 26 optional independent libraries: pdf-writer, lopdf, png, gif, zip, flate2, csv, image, tobj, stl_io, quick-xml, json, html5ever, markup5ever_rcdom, comrak, dxf, las, mp4, ruststep, calamine, rust_xlsxwriter, ply-rs, riff, id3, bstr, tiff (count must be read directly from the current manifest rather than trusted from prose). Preserve actual manifest library versions, flags, and defaults exactly in the new test-only composition package; its direct lower dependency remains semio-repo-test-host. No production dependency is added.
- Real contribution descriptor is **`✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json`**, not the obsolete `🔣️oracle.json` mentioned in source comments. It contains 13 oracle entries, 1 no-oracle decision, 6 comparison profiles, 4 host package entries. Twelve oracle entries point `hostPath` to the old native crate; Rust host entry enables `features: ["oracles"]`. Three Python distributions remain independently declared.
- Contributions are authored `🔮️oracles/🔣️.json` records discovered via taxonomy. No new hardcoded Stdio oracle descriptor generator is warranted. Keep oracle IDs/capabilities/comparison profiles/license/network/production-isolation evidence with their current artifact/plugin evidence owners, updating only the actual native host linkage. `hostPath` on entries and `oracleHostPackages[].path/package` must agree with the new composition crate.
- Existing artifact subset manifests also declare hostPath to the old crate; cross-plugin manifests often contribute it without `oracles` solely for law helpers. Split those consumers onto the lower `semio-repo-test-host::law` rather than enabling format libraries globally.

## Existing Generic Insertion Interfaces and Call Chain

`🧰️framework/🛍️products/🦑️repo/🔨️modules/🧪️test/🟦️.ts:622-640` defines owned `OracleHostPackage { implementation, package, path?, module?, features?... }`; `:649` defines `TestContribution`; `:721-753` reads authored descriptors; `:765-786` discovers taxonomy-named contributions; `:801-825` merges registry; `:833-835` resolves packages. Schema authority is `🧰️framework/🔨️modules/🧪️test/🧬️schema/🔣️.json:$defs/OracleHostPackage` (line 1963). Use this existing interface unchanged where possible.

`🧰️framework/🛍️products/🦑️repo/🔨️modules/🧪️test/🕸️dependencies/🟨️.mjs:34-38` selects *all applicable ancestor* contributions. The new composition's path may be declared by the existing owning plugin/subset contributions, which are ancestors of cases: there is no need to put a universal format-linked package into every S test through an S root descriptor. Avoid confusing a runtime package's location with the descriptor's evidence owner.

`🧰️framework/🛍️products/🦑️repo/🔨️modules/🧪️test/🖥️host/🏗️materialization/🟦️.ts:69-71` reads contributed packages; `:74-170` builds a cache-local Cargo host, merges equal package declarations/features and rejects divergent paths. Each owner-selected host package is linked by path; adapter is mounted, then `semio_repo_test_host::run_main(adapter::adapter())` runs its registration. `sut` activates only for the subject role. No format-specific materializer changes are required to relocate the composition crate.

Concrete before/after PDF chain: generated host → committed `📖️pdf/.../🧪️tests/➕️create-minimal-pdf/🦀️.rs:12` → old `semio_s_plugin_stdio_test_oracle::document::oracle_create_pdf/project_pdf` → optional independent pdf-writer/lopdf. After: same generated host/adapter/context/scenario/feature/corpus → new `semio_s_test_oracle_composition::document::*` → same independent implementation. Subset mutation adapters similarly replace the native crate import, retaining the same exact subset functions/KINDS and forward/inverse semantics. `Pdf` base subset oracle source itself `:49` imports `crate::document`; the higher composition's sibling module retains that owned interface.

`🧰️framework/🛍️products/🦑️repo/🔨️modules/🧪️test/🟨️.mjs:98-101,105-115` discovers applicable oracle package paths, computes native dependency closure and tasks. Thus source ownership/cache authority must include the higher authored root, the 72 mounted subset sources, family helper files and independent manifest declarations; relocating only the Cargo manifest will leave missing cache inputs. Existing exhaustive-cache-inputs test `🧪️tests/⚡️exhaustive-cache-inputs/🟦️.ts:79` hardcodes the old package input and must be rewritten for the actual new authority.

## Required New Authority and Tests Before Movement

1. Add a language-neutral composition schema/fixture in the new S owner with exact ordered contribution rows (artifact/standard/subset/source binding/export route), unique identities and exhaustive 72-entry match against real contribution evidence. Mechanism belongs in an existing neutral test infrastructure facet if reused, never framework code naming PDF/Stdio.
2. Assert each admitted source is a regular no-follow file beneath its actual leaf owner; composition-owned mounting literals resolve exactly once to it; no missing/duplicate/foreign source or undeclared export accepted. Root virtual workspace/ambient nearest manifest must not confer membership.
3. Validate descriptors with existing independent JSON-schema oracle (Ajv), and compare ordered admission, duplicate rejection, deletion/removal behavior to an independent simple implementation over the same language-neutral vectors. Explicit rejected cases: wrong leaf identity, duplicate source route, cross-owner synthetic source, unknown feature, symlink parent/leaf, missing contribution.
4. Preserve exact artifact oracle IDs, all test case IDs/features/tags, mutation catalog counts, fixture manifest provenance and corpus digests before/after. Every current forward/inverse/identity law retains an implementation and runtime registration; no declared oracle merely links while its handler disappears.
5. Pure oracle role must build/run without linking or invoking the subject. Production reachability must reject the higher composition crate and its third-party libraries from all production roots. `oracles` on/off behavior and independent dependency flags must match existing contract; do not silently broaden production feature closure.
6. Add neutral host integration tests for a contributed package at S composition scope, feature merging, conflicting path rejection, cache input closure and cancellation/progress. These tests use synthetic artifacts through the public contribution interface, not concrete Stdio names.
7. Re-run complete Rust source direction and existing dependency/test-layout/oracle isolation gates. Removing these 72 edges cannot certify the whole repository green while other known violations persist.

## Commands and Developer Entry Points

Existing verification commands, not executed in this audit:

- `bun nx run @semio-tech/repo-lib:test-rust-source-direction`
- `bun nx run @semio-tech/repo-lib:lint-rust-source-direction`
- `bun nx run @semio-tech/repo-test:test` — includes host/contribution/schema/layout tests through the existing permanent script router.
- `bun nx run @semio-tech/repo-test:lint`
- `bun nx run workspace:stdio-exhaustive` — root `📋️project.json:1279` depends on `stdio-long`, invokes existing `📜️script.ts stdio exhaustive`; `.vscode/launch.json:16675` already exposes `▶️stdio-exhaustive`.
- Existing taxonomy-discovered case `test-oracle` and `test-exhaustive` Nx targets exercise the actual committed adapters/third-party corpus. Select representative family pilots first, then all 72 admitted subset routes; do not manually invoke Cargo outside permanent routing.

New composition package commands must live solely in its `📜️script.ts`, project targets call that script, and new launch entries follow existing grouping/order. No standalone helper scripts. Register owner/package/task authority and source closure through the current catalog/taxonomy interfaces before attempting a compiler plan; do not infer a package merely from its emoji path.

✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/🪟️bmp/🏅️standards/🔖️v3/🪆️subsets/✳️any/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:21:      "hostPath": "✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust",
✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/🪟️bmp/🏅️standards/🔖️v3/🪆️subsets/✳️any/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:74:      "rationale": "📖️ A READER, and that is the whole point of registering it separately from `image-bmp-3-mutate` above.\n\nThat sibling entry is `cross-semio-implementation`: what a mutation SHOULD produce is computed by this owner's own `🦀️oracle.rs` (533 lines, whose own header states it 'deliberately mirrors' the production diff semantics), so both halves of that comparison descend from one reading of the format's specification.\n\nThis entry is a different mechanism. The expected state is not computed at all — it is COMMITTED, as the `after` half of a byte-reproducible fixture built directly by the standalone `image-bmp-codec` binary (`../🏭️generator/🔁️codec/📦️packages/🦀️rust`, depends on nothing but `image` 0.25's own `bmp` feature). `image` then decodes BOTH sides through its own public `BmpDecoder`/`BmpEncoder` surface, and the comparison is over what it recovered — no BITMAPINFOHEADER byte-offset parsing of this module's own, unlike the sibling `component.rs`. The probes say so in their own header: 'Everything here MARSHALS and READS; nothing here applies a mutation or predicts what one should.'\n\nOnly 4 of this subset's 5 declared `bmp-3-mutate` kinds are registered against this oracle. `change-header-fields` is not: reading `image-0.25.10`'s own vendored decoder/encoder source (`src/codecs/bmp/{decoder,encoder}.rs`) shows `BmpDecoder`'s `top_down`, `bit_count` and `colors_used` fields are private with no public accessor, and `BmpEncoder::encode_with_palette` hard-codes horizontal/vertical pixels-per-metre to `0`, 'colours important' to `0`, colour planes to `1`, compression to 'none', and always stores rows bottom-up — none of `change-header-fields`' twelve payload fields (`rowOrder`, `xPixelsPerMeter`, `yPixelsPerMeter`, `compression`, `colorsUsed` as a value distinct from palette length, `colorsImportant`, `headerSize`, `imageSize`, an exact `bitsPerPixel`) round-trips through `image`'s public BMP API in either direction. Only `width`/`height` do (via `BmpDecoder::dimensions`), and this subset's own committed production test scenario for `change-header-fields` (`../🧬️schema/🧬️mutations/📐️change-header-fields/🧪️tests/direct-behavior/🦠️mutation/🔣️.json`) exercises `width` paired with `xPixelsPerMeter`/`yPixelsPerMeter` together — exactly the fields this reader cannot see — so a width-only recipe would dodge the mutation's own real target fields rather than honestly witness it. See `oracleRequirements` below and the `bmp-3-mutate-uncarried` capability, and this subset's own `📓️bmp-v3-any-reader-oracle-retrofit.md` for the full source-level evidence trail.\n\n`productionReachable` is `false`: every non-test/non-scratch `Cargo.toml` in this repository that depends on `image` (`🎞️animate`, `🖍️draw`, `🧰️framework/🔨️modules/🗺️surface`, `🧰️framework/🛍️products/💻️os/🔨️modules/♾️infinite`, and the os renderer's wgpu target) enables only `png`/`jpeg`/`webp`/`gif` — none enables the `bmp` feature `image`'s BMP codec is gated behind. The only Cargo.toml in this repository enabling `image`'s `bmp` feature outside a scratch ticket directory is `✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust/Cargo.toml` itself, an `optional = true` dependency of the shared test-oracle host crate. `image`'s BMP support is therefore genuinely test-only in this repository today, which is a narrower claim than the sibling `image-bmp-3-mutate` entry's own `productionReachable: true` (with a `productionDebt` naming `🎞️animate`'s video engine and `🧰️framework/🔨️modules/🗺️surface`'s tiled-map as reachable-from sites) — that claim is about the `image` package as a whole being production-reachable for OTHER formats it does compile in for (png/jpeg/webp/gif), not about its BMP codec specifically, which is what this entry's capability is scoped to. Left as-is per this ticket's scope (leave `image-bmp-3-mutate` untouched); flagged here as a real, source-verified discrepancy rather than silently copied.",

✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/4️⃣1.4/🪆️subsets/🗄️a/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:21:      "hostPath": "✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust",
✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/4️⃣1.4/🪆️subsets/🗄️a/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:64:      "rationale": "📖️ A READER, over a corpus that had to be REBUILT before one could mean anything.\n\nThe previous generator imported `oracle_apply_mutation` and `project_conformance` from `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`, this repository's own crate, so the mutated bytes AND the expected projection of them were both ours and `lopdf` was a codec for our own answer. Registering a reader beside that corpus would have changed the label and nothing else.\n\n`🦀️lopdf-engine` depends on `lopdf` and nothing else: it lays out the seed, performs each mutation through lopdf's own public COS API, and reads the conformance axes back through it. It refuses to write a pair whose projection does not move, so a no-op cannot be committed as a fixture that would pass forever.\n\nThe two encryption kinds are `-uncarried`, not routed around: lopdf 0.44's writer takes its encryption path whenever the trailer carries `/Encrypt` and then needs the encryption state a genuine decryption would have recorded, so a synthetic encryption dictionary can be neither written nor read back. A writer-side limit, not a reader gap."

✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/4️⃣1.4/🪆️subsets/🖨️x/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:21:      "hostPath": "✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust",
✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/4️⃣1.4/🪆️subsets/🖨️x/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:64:      "rationale": "📖️ A READER, over a corpus that had to be REBUILT before one could mean anything.\n\nThe previous generator imported `oracle_apply_mutation` and `project_conformance` from `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`, this repository's own crate, so the mutated bytes AND the expected projection of them were both ours and `lopdf` was a codec for our own answer. Registering a reader beside that corpus would have changed the label and nothing else.\n\n`🦀️lopdf-engine` depends on `lopdf` and nothing else: it lays out the seed, performs each mutation through lopdf's own public COS API, and reads the conformance axes back through it. It refuses to write a pair whose projection does not move, so a no-op cannot be committed as a fixture that would pass forever.\n\nThe two encryption kinds are `-uncarried`, not routed around: lopdf 0.44's writer takes its encryption path whenever the trailer carries `/Encrypt` and then needs the encryption state a genuine decryption would have recorded, so a synthetic encryption dictionary can be neither written nor read back. A writer-side limit, not a reader gap."

The fixtures registered alongside are generated through that same second implementation (`apply_conformance_mutation` in `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`), so both halves of the comparison descend from one reading of the specification, and a misreading of it yields two agreeing wrong answers.\n\n`lopdf` discharges \"the result is a well-formed PDF\", NOT \"the mutation computed the right answer\". Only the second is what a mutation oracle is for. A required SUPPLEMENT, never a substitute.\n\nThe fixtures stay and are valuable: real PDF bytes, byte-reproducible, covering every declared kind of this conformance profile — exactly what a qualifying third-party reference gets pointed at when one exists.",
✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/7️⃣1.7/🪆️subsets/🗄️a/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:21:      "hostPath": "✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust",
✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/7️⃣1.7/🪆️subsets/🗄️a/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:64:      "rationale": "📖️ A READER, over a corpus that had to be REBUILT before one could mean anything.\n\nThe previous generator imported `oracle_apply_mutation` and `project_conformance` from `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`, this repository's own crate, so the mutated bytes AND the expected projection of them were both ours and `lopdf` was a codec for our own answer. Registering a reader beside that corpus would have changed the label and nothing else.\n\n`🦀️lopdf-engine` depends on `lopdf` and nothing else: it lays out the seed, performs each mutation through lopdf's own public COS API, and reads the conformance axes back through it. It refuses to write a pair whose projection does not move, so a no-op cannot be committed as a fixture that would pass forever.\n\nThe two encryption kinds are `-uncarried`, not routed around: lopdf 0.44's writer takes its encryption path whenever the trailer carries `/Encrypt` and then needs the encryption state a genuine decryption would have recorded, so a synthetic encryption dictionary can be neither written nor read back. A writer-side limit, not a reader gap."

✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/7️⃣1.7/🪆️subsets/🧾️vt/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:20:      "rationale": "🫥️ RECLASSIFIED, on the same ground as `gltf`, `png`, `jpg`, `bmp`, `tiff` and `avi`. `lopdf` is a real PDF codec and does real work — but what a MUTATION SHOULD PRODUCE is computed by this owner's own `🔮️oracles/🦀️.rs`, whose doc comments say it plainly: the forward and undo results are \"read out of the BYTES by the INDEPENDENT IMPLEMENTATION\". The fixtures registered alongside are generated through that same second implementation (`apply_conformance_mutation` in `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`), so both halves of the comparison descend from one reading of the specification, and a misreading of it yields two agreeing wrong answers.\n\n`lopdf` discharges \"the result is a well-formed PDF\", NOT \"the mutation computed the right answer\". Only the second is what a mutation oracle is for. A required SUPPLEMENT, never a substitute.\n\nThe fixtures stay and are valuable: real PDF bytes, byte-reproducible, covering every declared kind of this conformance profile — exactly what a qualifying third-party reference gets pointed at when one exists.",
✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/7️⃣1.7/🪆️subsets/🧾️vt/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:21:      "hostPath": "✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust",
✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/7️⃣1.7/🪆️subsets/🧾️vt/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:64:      "rationale": "📖️ A READER — and the reason this subset needed one is worth stating exactly, because the sibling entry looked qualifying and was not.\n\nThe previous fixture corpus was generated by a binary that imported `oracle_apply_mutation` and `project_conformance` from `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`, this repository's OWN crate. The mutated bytes and the expected projection of them were therefore both ours, and `lopdf` was reduced to a codec for our own answer: a shared misreading of the PDF specification would have produced two agreeing wrong answers and the test would still have passed. Registering a reader beside that corpus would have changed the label and nothing else.\n\nSo the corpus was REBUILT. `🦀️lopdf-engine` depends on `lopdf` and nothing else: it lays out the seed, performs each mutation through lopdf's own public COS API, and reads the conformance axes back through it. Nothing in this repository predicts what this reader judges.\n\nTwo of the eighteen kinds are registered `-uncarried` rather than routed around: lopdf 0.44's writer takes its encryption path whenever the trailer carries `/Encrypt` and then requires the encryption state a genuine decryption would have recorded, so a synthetic encryption dictionary can be neither written nor read back. That is a writer-side limit, not a reader gap."

✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🗿️artifacts/📖️pdf/🏅️standards/7️⃣1.7/🪆️subsets/🧱️base/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:65:      "rationale": "📖️ A READER, over a corpus that had to be REBUILT before one could mean anything.\n\nThe previous generator imported `oracle_apply_mutation` and `project_conformance` from `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`, this repository's own crate, so the mutated bytes AND the expected projection of them were both ours and `lopdf` was a codec for our own answer. Registering a reader beside that corpus would have changed the label and nothing else.\n\n`⚖️lopdf-engine` depends on `lopdf` and nothing else: it lays out the seed, performs each mutation through lopdf's own public COS API, and reads the conformance axes back through it. It refuses to write a pair whose projection does not move, so a no-op cannot be committed as a fixture that would pass forever.\n\nThe two encryption kinds are `-uncarried`, not routed around: lopdf 0.44's writer takes its encryption path whenever the trailer carries `/Encrypt` and then needs the encryption state a genuine decryption would have recorded, so a synthetic encryption dictionary can be neither written nor read back. A writer-side limit, not a reader gap."

✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗒️note/🗿️artifacts/🗒️note/🏅️standards/🔖️1/🪆️subsets/✳️any/🔮️oracles/🔣️.json:151:      "rationale": "📄️ `lopdf` is ALREADY an approved test-oracle in this repository (`🔒️dependencies.json` — capabilities `pdf-edit`/`pdf-parse`, linked into the very crate `../../../../../../../../✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/📦️packages/🦀️rust/Cargo.toml` this registration reuses); this entry is a second registration of that crate against `note-1-mutate` rather than a new dependency.\n\n`NoteIntoPdf::serialize` (`../🚪️io/📤️export/🧵️serializers/🗿️artifacts/📄️pdf/🔖️1.4/✳️any/🦀️.rs`) flattens the document's `title` (if any) followed by EVERY `Text` block's paragraph runs, space-joined, onto ONE `PdfPage.text` field of a single-page `PdfSnapshot` — real page geometry (`PageDoc`/`w`/`h`) and a real `encode_pdf`, never a stub. Two consequences read directly from that body: (1) `rename-note` IS witnessable here — the title is the first thing concatenated — even though it is invisible to both DXF and SVG. (2) exactly like the DXF path, `flatten_blocks` here is never filtered by `block_visible`, so `change-block-visible` on a Text block leaves the PDF text unchanged (the same cross-carrier inconsistency the DXF entry reports, independently confirmed on a second serializer). Position, block existence order beyond concatenation order, and every non-Text block kind (Ink/Image/Table/Math/Group) are invisible: the serializer never reads their fields at all."

The fixtures registered alongside are generated through that same second implementation (`apply_conformance_mutation` in `semio-s-plugin-stdio-test-oracle`), so both halves of the comparison descend from one reading of the specification, and a misreading of it yields two agreeing wrong answers.\n\n`lopdf` discharges \"the result is a well-formed PDF\", NOT \"the mutation computed the right answer\". Only the second is what a mutation oracle is for. A required SUPPLEMENT, never a substitute.\n\nThe fixtures stay and are valuable: real PDF bytes, byte-reproducible, covering every declared kind of this conformance profile — exactly what a qualifying third-party reference gets pointed at when one exists.",

## Neutral Law Extraction Details

Law root `✏️s/🔌️plugins/🗄️stdio/🔮️oracles/⚖️law/🦀️.rs:23` and `🧬️vector/🦀️.rs:10` currently import the test host externally. When moved *inside* that crate, use its own `crate::{parse_json, Json, Context}` interfaces. Root `:259-268` explicitly mounts vector and unit-test facets; move these together and recalculate every retained fixture include. `wire_operation` at `:251` is generic over the mutation and decoder/emitter callbacks; it does not implement any artifact format despite its Stdio-specific docstring. The host root `🧰️framework/🛍️products/🦑️repo/🔨️modules/🧪️test/🦀️.rs:23` already exports `Adapter`, `Context`, and `run_main`; adding neutral `law` below the same owned domain avoids a new runtime library or dependency. Existing oracle leaf `crate::law::*` consumers must switch directly to `semio_repo_test_host::law::*`; keeping `crate::law` as an alias in the S composition would retain an unnecessary compatibility facade. Existing law source-file mounting in other plugins must become that actual lower API, so generated adapter provenance and cache closures reflect the new dependency.

No existing permanent S-wide authored oracle composition package was found at the proposed path. That path requires explicit taxonomy/catalog admission and package/launch/task registration. Merely moving files into an unrecognized test collection would be a new ownership failure even if the physical direction regex accepts the path.
